Hi. Yeah, nice to meet you. Um, well, I guess what brought me in is work stuff. My supervisor is getting really mad because I keep leaving my station on the production floor, or like, popping up and walking around during our shift meetings when I'm supposed to just sit and stay put. 

Which is annoying because the chairs in that breakroom are terrible anyway. They're those hard metal folding ones, you know? My buddy Jason actually broke one last week just by leaning back, and they still haven't replaced it, so now there's like one less chair for everybody and you have to stand if you get there late. They really should just get the padded ones like they have in the front office. 

But anyway, yeah, my boss says me getting up is becoming a major safety problem. I don't even mean to do it, honestly. I just take off before I even think about it, and suddenly I'm halfway down the line. So yeah, they said I needed to come talk to somebody about it.

Well, I mean, it's been getting me into hot water for, like, a year and two months now. That's when I really tried to buckle down, you know? Oh, yeah, for sure. Even way back in elementary school, I was always that kid who just could not stay in my seat. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, it's definitely the moving around part. Like, when I try to stand still by the machine on the line, or if I have to sit through one of those long safety trainings, it literally feels like there's this pressure building up inside my body. I just have to move, or pace, or stretch, or go do something right then. I can't just stay put. 

Especially in those trainings. They make us sit in the breakroom and the chairs are those hard plastic ones that totally kill your back. Plus the AC is always blasting, even when it's raining outside. I complained to HR about it once, this lady named Brenda. She's always wearing these crazy bright floral blouses, like she's going to a luau or something. I asked her where she buys them and she said QVC! I didn't even know QVC was still a thing. My mom used to watch that channel all the time when I was little and she'd buy those weird porcelain dolls. They creeped me out so bad. They have those shiny glass eyes that look like they're following you around the room. I used to throw a towel over the one in my bedroom because I couldn't sleep with it staring at me. I'm a really light sleeper anyway, I always need a fan on for the white noise, but then I wake up freezing... 

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, which one is worse. Yeah, definitely the having to move around. The pressure just gets too crazy if I try to just stand still.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess my mind does wander off sometimes. Like when I'm doing the inventory logs or trying to read through those CNC training pages they gave us. I start out really trying to focus, I swear I do. But after a few minutes, it's like the numbers or the words just start blurring together on the page. And then I realize I've been staring at it but I didn't take any of it in, so I have to keep going back and starting over. 

Which is super annoying because the font they use for those manuals is so tiny. It's like size eight or something. My friend Jimmy—he works in shipping—he says they do it on purpose to save money on paper. Which I totally believe, because this company is so cheap. They won't even buy the good brand of toilet paper for the bathrooms. It's that awful single-ply stuff that feels like sandpaper. I started bringing my own rolls in my backpack, honestly. I get the Charmin Ultra Soft, the one with the bears in the commercials? Those commercials are hilarious, especially the one where the bear is singing. I saw this TikTok the other day where someone did a remix of the Charmin bear song with a Drake beat and it was so funny I actually spit my energy drink everywhere. I drink a lot of those Monster energy drinks, the white ones. They taste kinda like grapefruit, but sweeter. I tried the green one once and it made my heart race so fast I thought I was gonna pass out right there on the factory floor...

Wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, paying attention. Yeah, the words just blur together and I have to keep rereading the same lines over and over. It's really frustrating.

Has it always been like this since you were younger, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, it's been getting me into hot water for, like, a year and two months now. That's when I really tried to buckle down, you know? I was trying to tighten up my habits because I wanted to move up and get my forklift certification, and maybe CNC too. Which, honestly, driving a forklift seems so much better because you're actually doing something instead of just standing at a conveyor belt waiting for parts. My cousin drives a forklift at this huge warehouse, and he says he basically just listens to music all day while he drives around. He got these really nice noise-canceling headphones for Christmas, the ones that connect to your phone with Bluetooth? I was thinking about getting a pair, but they're so expensive, and I'd probably just lose them anyway. I lose everything. I lost my keys twice last week. But yeah, the getting up thing... it's been a real issue since I tried to get serious about the certifications about a year and a couple months ago.\n\nDoctor: When you were a kid, say in elementary school or middle school, did you have trouble sitting still in class? Did your teachers ever say you were fidgety or restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, yeah, for sure. Even way back in elementary school, I was always that kid who just could not stay in my seat. Like, I was constantly talking over the teacher and I'd get all these notes sent home because I was always blurting things out or just, you know, wandering around the room during lessons. \n\nMy mom used to get so mad about those notes. They were always written on this weird pink paper. Actually, it looked exactly like the carbon copy paper we use at the plant for the shipping manifests now. Which is so stupid because everything should just be on iPads or something, right? I mean, who uses carbon paper anymore? The ink gets all over your fingers. I literally ruined a perfectly good white hoodie last week because I touched a manifest and then wiped my hands on my stomach without thinking. It was one of those really soft ones too, from Target. I love their hoodies, they're always on sale in the men's section and they have way better pockets than the women's ones. The women's ones can barely fit a phone, let alone my vape and my keys. \n\nBut um, wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, school. Yeah, the teachers were always sending those pink notes home about me not sitting still and blurting stuff out.\n\nDoctor: Besides the trouble sitting still and blurting things out, did you also have problems in school with things like losing your homework, forgetting instructions, or having trouble finishing assignments?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I didn't really lose my homework or anything. I just... I didn't like doing it, but I don't think I forgot it. But honestly, I didn't even finish high school, so maybe I'm not the best person to ask about school stuff. I dropped out in 11th grade. I just couldn't stand being cooped up in those classrooms all day. It felt like a prison, you know? \n\nSpeaking of prisons, I was watching this documentary on Netflix the other night about this guy who escaped from a maximum-security place in New York. It was crazy. He used, like, a hacksaw blade hidden in some frozen hamburger meat to cut through the pipes. Can you believe that? Hamburger meat! It made me want to make burgers, actually, so I went to the store at like 2 AM to get some ground beef. But then I got there and the only place open was this sketchy gas station, and all they had were those pre-packaged hot dogs that look like they've been rolling on the heater for a week. I bought one anyway, and let me tell you, it was a mistake. I felt sick for two days. I had to call off work, which my boss was super annoyed about because we were already short-staffed. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Homework? Yeah, no, I didn't really have a problem with losing it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned losing your keys twice last week and losing things in general. Besides the sitting still issue, do you find it hard to keep track of things like bills, appointments, or important stuff at work? Or do you forget what people just told you pretty often?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I guess I forget normal stuff all the time. Like at work, I'll totally forget to clock a step on the checklist, or I won't answer my supervisor's text, or I'll walk all the way to the line and realize I left my safety glasses in my locker. And then they get mad because it looks like I just don't care, you know? But I really just forgot. \n\nActually, my locker is a total disaster right now. It's one of those skinny metal ones, and the door always sticks. You have to hip-check it just to get it open. My friend Sarah, she works on line three, she put these little magnetic wallpaper things in hers? It looks so cute, like little daisies. I tried to do that with mine, but I bought the wrong kind of magnets and they all just slid down to the bottom. I bought them on Amazon, and I swear, Amazon is getting so bad with their descriptions. You think you're buying one thing and then it shows up and it's completely different. Like last month, I ordered this phone case that was supposed to be waterproof because I dropped my last phone in the toilet at the gas station. Which, gross, I know. But anyway, the case came and it was literally just a piece of cheap plastic. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, keeping track of stuff. Yeah, I forget to bring my safety glasses and check off the lists all the time. It's super annoying.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned blurting things out in school and getting up from your station without thinking. Do you find yourself doing things on impulse without stopping to think about the consequences first? Can you give me an example of something impulsive you've done recently?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know. Like, I don't think I do things that are super crazy or anything. I mean, I guess I just do stuff when I want to do it, but I don't know if that's what you mean. \n\nLike, I don't know, I just bought a new car? Well, it's not new, it's a used Honda Civic, but it's new to me. I wasn't really planning on buying it, I was just driving by the lot and I saw it and it was this really pretty blue color. Like a sky blue? My grandma used to have a kitchen painted that exact color. She had all these little ceramic roosters everywhere too. It was so weird, but kind of cute. She'd always make us these huge breakfasts with pancakes and bacon and eggs. I miss her cooking so much. I tried to make pancakes from scratch the other day and I completely burned them. My fire alarm went off and my neighbor started banging on the wall. He's this older guy who's always complaining about noise. He even complained when I was just playing music on a Tuesday afternoon. Like, come on, it's 2 PM, I can listen to Morgan Wallen if I want to! \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know if I do stuff without thinking. I just kind of do what I feel like doing.\n\nDoctor: Right now, in your daily life, would you say the main problem is that you can't sit still and have to move around, or is it more that you have trouble focusing and remembering things?\n\nPatient: Oh, it's definitely the moving around part. Like, when I try to stand still by the machine on the line, or if I have to sit through one of those long safety trainings, it literally feels like there's this pressure building up inside my body. I just have to move, or pace, or stretch, or go do something right then. I can't just stay put. \n\nEspecially in those trainings. They make us sit in the breakroom and the chairs are those hard plastic ones that totally kill your back. Plus the AC is always blasting, even when it's raining outside. I complained to HR about it once, this lady named Brenda. She's always wearing these crazy bright floral blouses, like she's going to a luau or something. I asked her where she buys them and she said QVC! I didn't even know QVC was still a thing. My mom used to watch that channel all the time when I was little and she'd buy those weird porcelain dolls. They creeped me out so bad. They have those shiny glass eyes that look like they're following you around the room. I used to throw a towel over the one in my bedroom because I couldn't sleep with it staring at me. I'm a really light sleeper anyway, I always need a fan on for the white noise, but then I wake up freezing... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, which one is worse. Yeah, definitely the having to move around. The pressure just gets too crazy if I try to just stand still.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on a task or listen to someone, like your supervisor giving you instructions, do you find your mind wandering off, or is it more that you just can't sit still even when you're trying hard to pay attention?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess my mind does wander off sometimes. Like when I'm doing the inventory logs or trying to read through those CNC training pages they gave us. I start out really trying to focus, I swear I do. But after a few minutes, it's like the numbers or the words just start blurring together on the page. And then I realize I've been staring at it but I didn't take any of it in, so I have to keep going back and starting over. \n\nWhich is super annoying because the font they use for those manuals is so tiny. It's like size eight or something. My friend Jimmy—he works in shipping—he says they do it on purpose to save money on paper. Which I totally believe, because this company is so cheap. They won't even buy the good brand of toilet paper for the bathrooms. It's that awful single-ply stuff that feels like sandpaper. I started bringing my own rolls in my backpack, honestly. I get the Charmin Ultra Soft, the one with the bears in the commercials? Those commercials are hilarious, especially the one where the bear is singing. I saw this TikTok the other day where someone did a remix of the Charmin bear song with a Drake beat and it was so funny I actually spit my energy drink everywhere. I drink a lot of those Monster energy drinks, the white ones. They taste kinda like grapefruit, but sweeter. I tried the green one once and it made my heart race so fast I thought I was gonna pass out right there on the factory floor...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, paying attention. Yeah, the words just blur together and I have to keep rereading the same lines over and over. It's really frustrating.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, I mean, the moving around is definitely the biggest thing, but... honestly, both of them are messing things up for me pretty bad right now. Like, my supervisors have actually written me up a couple of times already. They say I'm causing safety issues and messing up the production line because I keep walking away from my station or getting distracted. And now the other people on my shift don't even want to partner with me when we do handoffs because they think I'm too scattered. 

It sucks because I was really hoping to get my CNC certification, but now my job is like, on the line. I really need this job, too. My rent just went up like fifty bucks a month. The landlord didn't even give us a full thirty days' notice, he just slipped this typed-up paper under the door. And the paper had a typo on it! He spelled "effective" with an 'a' instead of an 'e'. I showed it to my neighbor—the grumpy one who complained about my music—and even he rolled his eyes. He said he's thinking about moving to Florida because his sister lives down there in one of those retirement communities where they drive golf carts everywhere. I think driving a golf cart around all day would be so fun. I went mini-golfing once on a date and the guy tried to show me how to hold the putter, but he was standing way too close and he smelled like cheap cologne. Like that Axe body spray guys used to wear in middle school? It gave me such a headache. I ended up faking a stomach ache just so I could go home early... 

But yeah, I don't know, it's just... the moving around and the not focusing, it's really putting my job and my certification at risk. People are just getting really frustrated with me at work.

It's one of those skinny metal ones, and the door always sticks. You have to hip-check it just to get it open. My friend Sarah, she works on line three, she put these little magnetic wallpaper things in hers? It looks so cute, like little daisies. I mean, honestly, it's really hard to say if one is worse than the other. I guess maybe the moving around is the biggest thing, but the focusing part is terrible too. Like, I try to read the instructions, and it's just... I can't. It's like my brain just won't lock onto the words.

I used this cheap palette I got at the drugstore and the colors were so chalky. They didn't blend at all, they just looked muddy. She was always so strict about keeping things nice. She used to cover our living room furniture in plastic. Have you ever sat on plastic-covered furniture in the summer? It's the worst. Your legs just stick to it and when you try to get up, it makes this loud peeling sound. It's so embarrassing when you have friends over. 

Wait, what did you ask me? I'm sorry, I forgot what we were talking about again.

So yeah, they said I needed to come talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: How long has this problem with getting up and moving around been going on? Has it always been like this since you were younger, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, it's been getting me into hot water for, like, a year and two months now. That's when I really tried to buckle down, you know? I was trying to tighten up my habits because I wanted to move up and get my forklift certification, and maybe CNC too. Which, honestly, driving a forklift seems so much better because you're actually doing something instead of just standing at a conveyor belt waiting for parts. My cousin drives a forklift at this huge warehouse, and he says he basically just listens to music all day while he drives around. I was thinking about getting a pair, but they're so expensive, and I'd probably just lose them anyway. I lose everything. I lost my keys twice last week. Did your teachers ever say you were fidgety or restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, yeah, for sure. Even way back in elementary school, I was always that kid who just could not stay in my seat. Like, I was constantly talking over the teacher and I'd get all these notes sent home because I was always blurting things out or just, you know, wandering around the room during lessons. \n\nMy mom used to get so mad about those notes. They were always written on this weird pink paper. Actually, it looked exactly like the carbon copy paper we use at the plant for the shipping manifests now. Which is so stupid because everything should just be on iPads or something, right? I mean, who uses carbon paper anymore? The ink gets all over your fingers. I literally ruined a perfectly good white hoodie last week because I touched a manifest and then wiped my hands on my stomach without thinking. It was one of those really soft ones too, from Target. I mean, I didn't really lose my homework or anything. I just... I didn't like doing it, but I don't think I forgot it. But honestly, I didn't even finish high school, so maybe I'm not the best person to ask about school stuff. I dropped out in 11th grade. I just couldn't stand being cooped up in those classrooms all day. It felt like a prison, you know? \n\nSpeaking of prisons, I was watching this documentary on Netflix the other night about this guy who escaped from a maximum-security place in New York. It was crazy. He used, like, a hacksaw blade hidden in some frozen hamburger meat to cut through the pipes. Can you believe that? Hamburger meat! It made me want to make burgers, actually, so I went to the store at like 2 AM to get some ground beef. But then I got there and the only place open was this sketchy gas station, and all they had were those pre-packaged hot dogs that look like they've been rolling on the heater for a week. I bought one anyway, and let me tell you, it was a mistake. I felt sick for two days. Yeah, no, I didn't really have a problem with losing it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned losing your keys twice last week and losing things in general. Or do you forget what people just told you pretty often?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I guess I forget normal stuff all the time. Like at work, I'll totally forget to clock a step on the checklist, or I won't answer my supervisor's text, or I'll walk all the way to the line and realize I left my safety glasses in my locker. And then they get mad because it looks like I just don't care, you know? But I really just forgot. \n\nActually, my locker is a total disaster right now. It's one of those skinny metal ones, and the door always sticks. You have to hip-check it just to get it open. My friend Sarah, she works on line three, she put these little magnetic wallpaper things in hers? It looks so cute, like little daisies. I tried to do that with mine, but I bought the wrong kind of magnets and they all just slid down to the bottom. I bought them on Amazon, and I swear, Amazon is getting so bad with their descriptions. You think you're buying one thing and then it shows up and it's completely different. Like last month, I ordered this phone case that was supposed to be waterproof because I dropped my last phone in the toilet at the gas station. Which, gross, I know. But anyway, the case came and it was literally just a piece of cheap plastic. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, keeping track of stuff. Yeah, I forget to bring my safety glasses and check off the lists all the time. Like a sky blue? My grandma used to have a kitchen painted that exact color. She had all these little ceramic roosters everywhere too. It was so weird, but kind of cute. She'd always make us these huge breakfasts with pancakes and bacon and eggs. I miss her cooking so much. I tried to make pancakes from scratch the other day and I completely burned them. It's definitely the physical part. Like, it's not just that I'm bored. When I try to stand still by the machine on the line, or when they make us sit through those long training videos, it actually feels like there's this crazy pressure building up inside my body.
